<meta>This is the title page of an early 18th-century alchemical text. It establishes the work as a collection of "secrets" attributed to the "Great" and "Small" Peasants—pseudonyms common in the era to suggest that true wisdom comes from nature and simple labor rather than scholarly vanity.</meta>

### Hamburg,

->Published by the heirs of Gottfried Liebezeit, Bookseller.<-
->Printed by Friedrich Conrad Greflinger, 1705.<-

<summary>This title page introduces a 1705 alchemical compendium that claims to reveal the inner workings of nature through a mystical vision. It specifically warns readers against fraudulent claims of creating the "elixir of life" or potable gold without the authentic Philosopher's Stone.</summary>
<keywords>Alchemy, The Great Peasant, The Small Peasant, Philosopher's Stone, Potable Gold, Cabala, Natural Philosophy, Hamburg, Gottfried Liebezeit</keywords>

--- Page 6 ---
<meta>This page serves as a frontispiece or introductory leaf following the title page. It features authoritative maxims from legendary figures in the alchemical tradition, intended to establish the theoretical foundation of the work. The ghosting noted in the OCR is a common artifact in 18th-century manuscripts where ink from the previous page ("The Open Chest..." and "Wednesday") has bled through the thin paper.</meta>

### Hermes.

*In the upper sphere, in the middle of the fountain, is a vein which is the first rule of the philosophers.* <note>original: "In superiori Sphæra est in medio fontis vena, quæ est Philosophorum Regula prima." Hermes Trismegistus is the mythical founder of alchemy; here, the "fountain" and its "vein" likely symbolize the source of the "Universal Spirit" or the "mercury of the wise" that powers the alchemical work.</note>

### Arros. <note>Arros (often identified as Arisleus) is a traditional character in alchemical dialogues like the "Turba Philosophorum," frequently used to voice established chemical truths.</note>

*All metals, regarding their purity, are of the same substance; they differ only by impurity, and by a greater or lesser degree of digestion.* <note>original: "Metalla omnia quoad puritatem sunt ejusdem substantiæ, differunt tantum impuritate, majori vel minori digestione." Alchemists believed that all metals were "growing" in the earth to become gold; "digestion" refers to the natural heat of the earth that matures base metals like lead into precious ones over centuries.</note>

<summary>This page presents two foundational alchemical axioms attributed to Hermes and Arros. They emphasize the existence of a primary "first rule" or source of wisdom and the theory that all metals share a single fundamental substance, differing only in their level of maturity or "digestion."</summary>
<keywords>Hermes Trismegistus, Arros, alchemical digestion, purity of metals, first rule, universal substance, 18th-century alchemy</keywords>

--- Page 7 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the authoritative axioms of the previous page to the author's personal address to the reader. The signature mark "a 2" indicates this is the second leaf of the introductory section (the preface). The author is establishing his credibility by framing his interest in alchemy as a pious study of God's creation rather than a commercial or professional pursuit.</meta>

## To the Christian Reader
### God’s Grace be with you first.

Although I have never applied my own hands to **alchemical** <term>Alchimistisch: relating to alchemy, the ancient study of transforming matter and finding the "Universal Medicine"</term> matters—since, as you should know, such things are not part of my **profession** <term>Beruff: a person's formal calling or vocational status</term>—I nevertheless find no small pleasure and delight in reaching a true **knowledge** <term>Erkäntnuß: the deep, experiential understanding of a subject</term> of the genuine **powers** <term>Kräfften: the inherent virtues or medicinal properties found in nature</term> of the **creatures** <term>Geschöpffen: in this historical context, "creatures" refers to all things created by God, including minerals, plants, and elements</term> of Almighty GOD. For since I also, at times out of **necessity** <term>Nothdurfft: pressing need or unavoidable circumstance</term>, occasionally also

<summary>This page begins the author's preface, where he introduces himself to the reader with a humble disclaimer. He explains that while he is not a professional alchemist, his study of the "powers of God's creatures" stems from a pious desire to understand the natural world.</summary>
<keywords>Christian reader, alchemical study, divine creation, natural philosophy, vocation, knowledge of God, 18th-century preface</keywords>

# The Opened Chest
->of the greatest Secrets of<-
->Nature.<-
->Firstly:<-
## The Great Builder.
<note>original: "Der grosse Baur." This term refers to God as the "Great Tiller" or "Creator" who fosters growth and prosperity.</note>
->The Prosperity from Him who is the<-
->Beginning and the End.<-

The holy Apostle James says in Chapter 1: "Every good gift and every perfect gift comes down from above, from the Father of Lights, with whom there is no variation or shadow of turning between light and darkness." <note>James 1:17</note> Paul speaks in 1 Corinthians 3: "I have planted, Apollos has watered; but God gives the growth."

Alanus the Philosopher: <note>Likely referring to Alain de Lille, a 12th-century scholar whose name was often attached to alchemical texts.</note> "Son, set your heart more upon God than upon the <term>Art<note>In this context, "the Art" refers to Alchemy, specifically the "Great Work" of transforming matter.</note>, for it is a gift from God; and to whom He wills, He shares it. Therefore, find peace and joy in God, and so you shall possess the Art." Alphidius: <note>An authority frequently cited in alchemical literature, often identified as an Arab philosopher.</note> "Son, you should know that you cannot attain this Art unless you have first purified your heart and mind before God, and heartily reconciled yourself with Him."

<summary>This introductory page frames alchemy not as a purely material pursuit, but as a divine gift dependent on the practitioner's spiritual purity. It uses biblical citations and references to legendary alchemical authorities like Alanus and Alphidius to emphasize that "the Art" is granted only by God's grace.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, divine grace, secrets of nature, James 1:17, 1 Corinthians 3, Alanus de Insulis, Alphidius, spiritual purity, The Great Builder</keywords>

# The Opened Chest of Nature.
<meta>catchword: cirten/</meta>

“When God then sees that you are of a sincere and well-meaning mind, He will out of grace let you master this <term>Art<note>In alchemical literature, "the Art" refers to the Great Work—the process of creating the Philosopher's Stone or achieving transmutation.</note>. Dionysius Zacharius <note>A 16th-century French alchemist known for his autobiographical accounts of the alchemical pursuit.</note>, folio 69. It will behoove you that you first, with patience and constancy, untiringly read the <term>Philosophers<note>The "Philosophers" refers to the historical lineage of alchemical authors whose texts were considered the foundation of the craft.</note> before you stretch out your hand to the work, always asking God for His grace and understanding; for no one comes to this Art through luck or by chance, but much rather through prayer than through other means—yet means must still be employed.

The Great King and Philosopher Hermes in the book *On the Oneness of Being* <note>original: "Rex magnus & Philosophus Hermes in lib. de nuitate entis". This likely refers to a text attributed to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founding father of Hermeticism and alchemy.</note>: Direct yourself entirely and completely toward that which is above you; lift the wings of your understanding to the radiance of the higher substance. Then you shall behold with your eyes—both inwardly and outwardly—their innumerable and high beauties and another light which surpasses all lights. You will be filled with wonder and pay no heed to all the works of the world. You will even, within your heart, desire and choose death before its appointed time, <term>chastening<note>The author uses the language of religious asceticism to describe the alchemical process of purification.</note> and killing your body.

Beyond that, however, you will deny and hate your own soul, praising the King of all kings and the God of Glory with glorious and beautiful songs of praise. And the Word of the Father, who loved us so much and is the Father’s Heart, we should worship and love with all our heart and soul, marvelling at so great a treasure, glorifying—

<summary>This page argues that alchemy is a spiritual discipline requiring deep study of the "Philosophers" and divine intervention rather than mere chance. It features a mystical passage attributed to Hermes Trismegistus describing the ecstatic state and detachment from the material world required to perceive the "higher substance."</summary>
<keywords>Dionysius Zacharius, Hermes Trismegistus, alchemical study, prayer, spiritual purification, divine light, the Art</keywords>

# The Great Farmer.
<meta>catchword: [to pra]ctice</meta>

...practice, so that we may finally be deemed worthy of the **communion of God** <note>original: "Gemeinschafft GOttes"</note>, and be filled with grace through Him, to whom be praise and honor from eternity to eternity, Amen.

# The Great Farmer

Now follow the main points, wherein the true foundation is truly brought to light, so that one might even grasp it; and it is easy and not difficult, even though some claim otherwise. Previously, as much as length and necessity required, the high <term>Secret</term> <note>original: Arcanum; a deep mystery or hidden alchemical secret</note> was treated <term>in a general way</term> <note>original: generali modo</note>; therefore follows the thorough instruction and proof <term>specifically</term> <note>original: in specie</note> and in particular. In this section, it will first be necessary to reveal at length the <term>Generation</term> <note>original: Generaturam; the process of birth or formation in nature</note> or birthing of <term>minerals</term> and <term>metals</term>. From this, <term>consequently</term> <note>original: consequenter</note>, the proof of the true <term>Matter</term> <note>original: Materien</note> or <term>Subject of the Stone</term> <note>original: Subjecti Lapidis; the raw starting material for the Philosopher's Stone</note> shall be described, proven, and demonstrated with the promised circumstances, <term>Requirements</term> <note>original: Requisitis</note>, and related matters. These primarily shall and must proceed from the course of Nature—namely, how the <term>metals</term> and other such things have their origin therein—which every true <term>Philosopher</term> <note>original: Philosophus; here meaning an alchemist and seeker of natural wisdom</note> and artist must follow, since all <term>Philosophers</term> write and cry out according to Nature.

### The Opened Cabinet of Nature

...truth and in fact it is not otherwise. Now it follows quite properly from what and by what means Nature generates or gives birth to the **Metals** <term>Metalla</term> in the **wombs of the earth**;

☞ Regarding this, you should be informed that Nature has her passages and her veins in the earth, which drink, lick, drip, and yield salty, clear, and murky waters—as can be seen in **mines** <note>original: Bergwercken</note>, where sharp, salty waters are always dripping. When these salty waters press down from above (for all heavy things sink toward the depths), they are met by the **Sulphurous vapors** <note>original: Sulphurische Dünste</note> rising from below **from the center of the earth** <note>original: ex centro terræ</note>. 

If it is found that the salty waters are pure and clear, and the Sulphurous vapors are also pure, and they meet and encounter one another, then a good metal is produced. If not, an impure metal is produced, upon which Nature must work for 1,000 years, more or less, before she makes it perfect; this is due to the impurity of either the **Mercurial** <note>original: Mercurialischen</note> salty waters or the Sulphurous impure vapor. 

When these two receive one another in a closed crevice or stone, a moist, thick, oily vapor rises from them both due to the actual heat of Nature. This vapor settles because it has no air (otherwise it would flee away), and from this vapor there then becomes a **mucilaginous** <term>mucilagische</term> <note>A thick, glue-like or viscous substance; in alchemy, this represents an intermediate state of matter between liquid and solid</note> or...

# The Great Peasant.

...demonstrate, explain, and prove with arguments and reasons.

The highly wise and spiritual man **Arnaldus de Villanova** <note>a famous 13th-century Catalan physician and alchemist</note> sets forth in his **Book of the Flowers of Flowers** <note>original: *liber Florum Florum*</note> the first irrefutable argument and proof with these words: everything that grows also multiplies, as seen in trees, grain, and all things. Likewise: **metals** <term>Metalla</term> grow; therefore, they can also be **multiplied** <term>multiplicirt</term> and increased like other things. That they grow is seen through experience, as cited above: often a mine in which the ore was found to be still incomplete <note>referring to "unripe" or immature ore that has not yet reached its metallic state</note> is filled back in and left for nature to grow toward perfection; and all experienced **miners** <term>Bergleute</term> know this. Furthermore, Almighty God himself says that in the creation all things should have an increase, where he says in **Genesis 1** <note>original: *Gen. 1.*</note>: "Let every thing bring forth after its kind and multiply within itself," and of this there is no doubt. A distinguished **Philosopher** <term>Philosophus</term> who possessed the **Stone** <term>Lapidem</term> <note>the Philosopher's Stone</note>, namely **Dionysius**, writes thus on **page 78**: "Everything that is ordained for **perfection** <term>Perfection</term> and left incomplete because of a lack of **cooking** <term>Kochung</term> <note>the alchemical process of heat and digestion over time</note> can be brought to perfection through subsequent cooking."

# The Great Peasant.

If one puts **metallic salts** <note>original: *Salis metallici*</note> into our water, it becomes like a white milk; but if one puts a lot into it, it becomes thick like butter, allowing itself to be smeared like grease or the like. I have rightly wished to remind you of this, so that you should have no doubt regarding the materials; and this will be demonstrated to you with the help of the sole Creator. **Johannes Chrysippus Fanianus** <note>A 16th-century Italian legal scholar and alchemical writer known for "On the Art of Alchemy."</note>, a powerful philosopher <note>original: *Philosophus*</note> who still lives (as I am informed) and is said to possess the **Stone** <note>original: *Lapidem*, referring to the Philosopher's Stone.</note>, but calls himself by that name so that he might not be recognized; he speaks thus: The hand of the **physician** <note>original: *Medici*</note> is required for the sick and not for the healthy; likewise, the hand of the **philosopher** <note>original: *Philosophi*</note> is required for the lesser and imperfect metals, and not for the precious and **perfect** <note>original: *perfecten*, referring to gold and silver</note> ones. 

***Magnesia*** <term>Magnesia</term> <note>In this context, not the modern element magnesium, but a traditional alchemical name for the "First Matter" or a crude mineral substance.</note> is what our **material** <note>original: *Materia*</note> is commonly called, which in the Chaldean language <note>Often used by alchemists to refer to Aramaic or ancient mystical terminology.</note> means "unmoved lead"—that is to say, lead that is still exactly as Nature **prepared** <note>original: *præpariret*</note> it, and has not yet passed through the hands of men, much less entered the fire. **Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary father of alchemy.</note> says: "In the metals lies the whole science, but not in the perfect ones, rather in the imperfect ones." **Ripley** <note>Sir George Ripley (c. 1415–1490), an influential English alchemist and author.</note> in his *Axioms* <note>original: *axiomatib.*</note> folio 8: "Do not believe the deceivers, for our **sulfur** <note>original: *Sulphur*</note> and **mercury** <note>original: *Mercurius*</note> are found only in the **imperfect things** <note>original: *Imperfectis*</note> or imperfect metals." *The Sound of the Trumpet* <note>original: *Clangor Bucc.*, referring to the alchemical treatise *Clangor Buccinae*.</note> folio 475: "If you seek a **medicine** <note>original: *Medicin*</note> that is to generate metals, you must seek it from the metals..."

...not neglect searching. ☞ *Rosarium Philosophorum* <note>original: *Ros.*; a famous 16th-century anthology of alchemical texts.</note> folio 379: It is **Saturn** <note>In alchemy, Saturn is the symbol and name for lead, considered the "oldest" and most base of the metals.</note> who tears my limbs apart. # The Great Farmer. #

...impure **Sun, Moon, and Mercury.** <note>original: *Sol, Luna und Mercurius*; the alchemical symbols for gold, silver, and quicksilver.</note> ☞ **Isaac Hollandus** <note>Johannes Isaac Hollandus, a famous (possibly legendary) 15th-century Dutch alchemist known for his works on mineral secrets.</note> in his *Book of Minerals* where he discusses our **Saturn** <note>In alchemy, Saturn is the code name for lead.</note>, says: "it is well worth contemplating that a metallic and Saturnian **body** <note>original: *Corpus*</note> exists which one can easily **dissolve** <note>original: *solviren*</note> and **putrefy** <note>original: *putrificiren*; to break down or rot, a necessary stage in alchemical transformation</note>; whoever knows its right **preparation** <note>original: *Præparation*</note> has good reason to be joyful." **Mercury** <note>Referring here to a legendary author or the personified spirit of quicksilver.</note> says: "You should know that our impure **body** <note>original: *Corpus*</note> is lead." *The Assembly of Philosophers* <note>original: *Turba Philosophorum*; one of the oldest and most influential Latin alchemical texts, written as a record of a debate between ancient philosophers.</note> folio 268. I say regarding **lead ore** <note>original: *Bleyertz*</note>: thus, everything must come from it and happen through it. **Rosinus** <note>original: *Rosin.*; likely referring to Zosimos of Panopolis, a 3rd-century alchemist.</note> folio 270: I tell you that our sulfurous, **living water** <note>A term for a powerful alchemical solvent or the "mercury of the philosophers."</note> is extracted from lead ore, which accomplishes everything. The same author: You should know that the **spirit of silver** and all whiteness must be **composed** <note>original: *componirt*</note> from lead ore in such a way that the **Moon** <note>original: *Luna*; the symbol for silver.</note> is a white stone (**salt of metals** <term>Sal metallorum</term>) without any blemish or impurity. That is a beautiful saying, in which he says that the **Moon** or **Moon-essence** <note>original: *Lunaria*; a symbolic name for the silver principle or a specific alchemical substance.</note> is in the lead ore; thus the white **Lunar salt** should be **extracted** <note>original: *extrahirt*</note> from it. It is indeed true: the whiteness that shines there is drawn from the **Saturnian body** <note>the lead substance</note>; it is beautifully clear, and one sees no **Moon** <note>silver</note> in it. But when one throws it upon a hot plate of **Venus** <note>Copper</note> or **Mars** <note>Iron</note>, it melts like wax, and the **spirit** <note>original: *Spiritus*</note> of life flies away above, and leaves the **Moon-essence** <note>original: *Lunariam*</note> lying quite beautifully behind it. This is very much to be wondered at, for "The **white Moon** <note>original: *alba Luna*</note> of which **Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the mythical founder of the hermetic and alchemical tradition.</note> speaks, is not..."

# The Great Peasant.

[which] provides / you would not be able to deny / that the same and no other is the true, authentic **material of the Stone** <note>original: *Materia des Lapidis*; the fundamental substance used to create the Philosopher's Stone</note>. Seeing as all **metals** <note>original: *Metalla*</note> originate and grow from it.

Now we wish to come closer / and speak of the most proximate materials: For there have been some **philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophi*; here referring to alchemical practitioners</note> / who from a whole pound received barely half a **Loth** <note>A traditional German unit of weight, roughly equivalent to 15 grams or half an ounce</note> / that was suitable for the Work / as *Clangor* <note>Referring to *Clangor Buccinae* (The Sound of the Trumpet), an influential alchemical treatise</note> testifies on folio 478. And there is a great advantage in this / because Nature has placed more **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurii*; the "living" spirit of the metal, not necessarily common quicksilver</note> or **salt-nitre** <note>original: *Salis nitri*; saltpeter, often viewed as a universal celestial salt in alchemy</note> in one than in another / and has also cooked one material more or longer than another / from which it is well to consider / that more lies hidden within those [materials] / upon which Nature has worked the most / but which have not yet stepped beyond their **limit** <note>original: *Termin*; the point of "fixation" where a mineral becomes too hard or "dead" to be easily transformed</note> / otherwise it would be too difficult to dissolve. Regarding this, **Flamel** <note>original: *Flamellus*; Nicolas Flamel, the famous 14th-century French scribe and legendary alchemist</note> says on folios 152 and 153 / that this is the most hidden secret / from which internal thing it can or must most immediately be taken / as explained by **Arnald of Villanova** <note>original: *Arnol. de vill. nov.*; a prominent 13th-century physician and alchemical author</note> in his *Rosary* <note>original: *Rosarium Philosophorum* (The Rose Garden of the Philosophers), a landmark text on the alchemical process</note> on folio 404. There are several intermediate materials / among which one is more purified by Nature than the others / also cooked and **digested** <note>original: *digerirt*; the alchemical process of gentle, continuous heat, mimicking the warmth of the womb or the earth</note> more and longer / and these are better and closest to the Work. I will set down some here / which are known to me and which I have **tested** <note>original: *probirt*</note> / I will also send some over and name the locations /

<meta>catchword: as</meta>

<summary>The author argues that certain raw materials are superior for the Great Work because Nature has already partially refined them through "cooking" and "digestion" within the earth. He cites authorities like Nicolas Flamel and Arnald of Villanova to support the idea that choosing a "proximate" material rich in Mercury saves the alchemist significant labor.</summary>

I admonish you, dear brother in Christ, who receives this instruction of mine / that you should fear God’s judgment / for you must give an account for any misuse / and yet you must keep such high secrets hidden / they are *Arcana* <note>original: *Arcana*; secret things</note>, and *Arca* <note>original: *Arca*</note> means a chest / where one should keep them inside / preserve them, and hide them from false people / when that happens / then dear God will also give His blessing to it / and there is no doubt / if the gentleman is of a good intention / his desire will succeed well for him / for the other points will be written just as clearly. Regarding secrecy, I have no doubt / otherwise God is not part of the game / and a true faith and loyalty should be present. You have understood enough from the cited passages <note>original: *allegatis locis*</note> of the books of the Philosophers / that they call the material <note>original: *materiam*</note> lead <note>original: *plumbum*</note>, graphite or lead-ore <note>original: *plumbaginem*</note>, litharge <note>original: *Lytargyrum*; a secondary mineral form of lead oxide</note>, and the lead of bronze <note>original: *plumbum æris*</note>, and all these things agree / although one [philosopher] may have had a more proximate material <note>original: *materiam*; a substance closer to the finished goal</note> than the other / yet it can be made just as well from the more remote <note>original: *Remotiore*</note> or most distant substance / but with greater effort and labor: one contains more Mercury <note>original: *Mercurii*; the transformative "spirit" of the metal</note> or metallic salt <note>original: *salis metallici*</note> than the other / but as said / they all aim for one purpose / the least [of these minerals] will [be found] at Saint

Thus you have / my dear friend / the instruction not only regarding the concordances <term>concordances: points of agreement or harmonies between the writings of different alchemical authors</term> of the philosophers / but also the materials. The eternal Almighty GOD grant / that you may use it to His honor and for the benefit of your neighbor.

How one should proceed further / that will follow in the second part; this treasure is not to be paid for with any gold / as Solomon says in his Wisdom in the 7th Chapter / and silver is to be counted as meager sand against it.

Other matters regarding the GUR <term>GUR: a term used by miners and alchemists for a "primordial" or unripe mineral substance, often described as a milky or gelatinous earth found in rock fissures</term> and the first matter <note>original: *prima Materia*; the fundamental, undifferentiated substance from which all metals and minerals were thought to grow</note> will also be shown / and there is absolutely no doubt / for I would have seen and experienced so much / that I (if it were possible) could also prove it with the deed / but it cannot be. Thus you have, my dear friend, the entire treatise <note>original: *Tractatum*</note> completely of the whole material / in which all philosophers agree.

It would have been somewhat shorter / but because I did not know whether the heir <note>original: *Hæres*</note> knows Latin / or not / I have set it down twice / albeit with difficulty / my son / only look up the books of the philosophers / you will find it no differently / although I can now prove the whole operation <note>original: *totam Operationem*; the complete sequence of steps in the alchemical work from start to finish</note> with the deed / God be praised / and

In this point, the best thing is to make the *concordant philosophers* <note>original: *Philosophos concordantes*; referring to different alchemical authorities whose writings agree on the fundamental secrets of the "Great Work"</note> harmonize and agree with one another; for the truth resides only in the metallic root <term>metallic root: the fundamental, primordial substance or "seed" from which all metals are thought to grow within the earth</term>, as the Count *Trevisan* <note>Bernard of Treviso (1406–1490), a famous Italian alchemist known for his search for the Philosopher's Stone</note> testifies on pages 14 and 16, the *Rosary of the Philosophers* <note>original: *Rosar.* / *Rosarium Philosophorum*; a seminal 16th-century alchemical text</note> on page 36, and *Flamel* <note>Nicolas Flamel, the legendary French alchemist</note> on page 147. *Reuchlin* in *On the Miraculous Word* <note>original: *Reuchl. de verbo mirifico*; a work by Johannes Reuchlin concerning the mystical power of sacred names</note> on folio 100 says: an evident proof of the truth is the *concordance* or harmony.

Conversely, however, the foundation of lies is *discord* <note>original: *discordia*</note> or disagreement; I shall leave it at that for now. He who does not allow himself to be satisfied with these proofs will never be helped.

> When the other points follow in due course, then it shall first truly shine, and a miraculous play will be seen. But I pray, and still protest for God’s sake, that one should let this my *treatise* <note>original: *Tractatum*</note> come before no one else. What you do not understand, I will explain as much as possible hereafter; but consult with no others, but rather pray diligently. Honor, praise, and glory be to Him who lives from eternity to eternity, Amen. Keep **GOD** before your eyes, pray, read, and work <note>A reference to the famous alchemical motto: *Ora, Lege, Lege, Relege, Labora et Invenies* (Pray, read, read, read again, work, and you shall find)</note>; **GOD** will help you, as He helped me.

# Part Two.

## THE LILY AMONG THORNS.
<note>original: *LILIUM INTER SPINAS*; a biblical phrase from the Song of Solomon often used in alchemy to represent the pure philosophical matter emerging from the "thorns" of base nature.</note>

King Solomon <note>Solomon was traditionally revered by alchemists as a primary master of the "Great Work" and a possessor of the Stone.</note> in the seventh chapter of the Book of Wisdom [says]: when I desired and prayed, prudence was given to me, and the spirit of wisdom came upon me; this I preferred before scepters and thrones, and I esteemed riches as nothing when compared to her; nor did I consider precious gems as comparable to her.

Likewise, all [Gold] <note>original: *Sol* (the Sun); the alchemical symbol for gold</note> compared to her is like a little sand, and before her [Silver] <note>original: *Luna* (the Moon); the alchemical symbol for silver</note> is to be regarded as mud.

I loved her above health and beauty, and preferred to have her rather than the light, because her radiance is never extinguished.

But with her all good things came to me at once, and

through her works immense riches resulted. For she is an inexhaustible treasure for men, which those who use enter into friendship with *God*, being commended for the gifts of learning. For she is a breath <note>original: *Vapor*; a mist or steam</note> of the power of God, and a pure emanation of the glory of the Almighty, etc. <note>This concludes the passage from the Wisdom of Solomon 7:11–25, which frames the "Stone" or the goal of alchemy as a divine gift of Wisdom rather than mere material wealth.</note>

->After the Clouds, the Sun.<-
<note>original: *Post Nubila Phoebus*; a popular Latin motto in alchemy signifying that enlightenment or success follows a period of darkness and failure.</note>

There was once an old proverb or saying: after great suffering, great joy usually follows. And likewise the opposite <note>original: *Sic & è contrà*</note>. Unfortunately, it happened to me just so a few years ago: it also happens to others who, when they have first begun without a true basis or foundation, fare almost as I shall recount at length. For when I believed I held the whole world in my hand, I obtained less than nothing. Because my glass <note>The alchemical vessel, often called the "Philosopher's Egg," in which the transformation was meant to occur.</note>—in which I had placed all my hopes for prosperity—shattered with a great crack; the matter <note>The chemical substance or "Philosophical Mercury" being prepared.</note> splashed my silent philosophers <note>original: *mutos Philoſophos*; a term for alchemical books, which "speak" to the reader without sound.</note> and books here and there, to my great loss and disadvantage. I must remain silent about further reports of this for now, except that I was very stiffened, silenced, and terrified by this unexpected misfortune—to such an extent that, out of mourning and anxiety, I did not know where I was, what I had intended, or how I [should proceed] further.

...should do; for all my joy, pleasure, and delight had transmuted and changed into nothing but poison and bitter gall, and not into gold and silver (as I had hoped). When I finally came to myself a little, I first considered my great damage and disadvantage; I began to lament and present the same with hot tears, bended knees, and heartfelt sighs to Him who lives from eternity to eternity (for God gives and takes as it well pleases Him, and to whom He wills), first praying that He would now intimately take me up and have mercy on me, that if it were possible, He would guide, lead, and show me the right way to the mirror of His Majesty through the Spirit of Truth and Wisdom. I comforted myself also by the fact that *Dionysius* and *Zacharias* <note>Likely referring to Pseudo-Dionysius the Areopagite and the 16th-century alchemist Denis Zachaire, who famously recorded his many failures before achieving success.</note> say that even most *Philosophers* <note>original: *Philosophi*; here meaning alchemists, who were known as "Natural Philosophers."</note> erred greatly in the beginning, and yet eventually arrived at a happy and profitable end.

Now, as I had troubled myself at length with these sorrowful thoughts, this doubtful question moreover occurred to me: Whether the Almighty God would still give, impart, and make known such a high *Secret* <note>original: *Secretum*; the hidden knowledge of the Philosopher's Stone or the mastery of nature.</note> to us poor sinners who live now in these last and evil times. After many reflections and stirrings, I finally decided within myself a...

...such a conclusion: that those very people who possessed this *Mystery* <note>original: *Mysterium*; the secret knowledge of alchemy.</note> before us were also sinners, and they obtained and received it not through their own merit, but through grace. Therefore, someone now who conducts himself piously and keeps God before his eyes could just as well receive it from the dear God through grace, and become a master of it, just as they did. Because of this, I took courage once again and bravely set to work upon my <term>Philosophers</term> <note>original: *Philosophos*; here referring to the authors of alchemical texts.</note>, <term>Magi</term> <note>original: *Magos*; wise men or practitioners of natural magic.</note>, and <term>silent Kings</term> <note>original: *mutos Reges*; a reference to the symbolic figures in alchemical imagery who "speak" through pictures rather than words.</note>, and especially the Count of Trevis <note>Bernard of Trevisan (1406–1490), a famous Italian alchemist whose writings emphasize the need for patience and the grace of God.</note>. ☞ For although I had diligently read through his work many times before, I could never grasp a certain foundation from it. At first I was startled, but then as I looked closer, the eyes of my understanding were opened. I could now clearly see and understand that which I was previously blind to, and for which I had longed for so long. ☞ I therefore rejoiced heartily and thanked God, adding a prayer that He would instruct me further as to what means I might use to reach the completion of the *High Work* <note>The *Magnum Opus*, or the creation of the Philosopher's Stone.</note>. I soon resolved to travel in search of the *Matter* (although it is indeed found everywhere), so that I might find the more immediate—

This is explained by George Ripley <note>Sir George Ripley (c. 1415–1490), an influential English alchemist and author of *The Compound of Alchymy* or the *Twelve Gates*.</note> in his *Axioms of the 12 Gates*, and also by Flamel <note>Nicolas Flamel (1330–1418), the legendary French scrivener and alchemist.</note> on pages 126 and 150 at the end, in these words:

☞ Now, while I was on my way, and my heart was full of such thoughts, it happened that between two mountains I encountered a fine, honorable, old Farmer. He was clothed in a long gray cloak or coat; on his hat he had a black band; at his neck, a white field-badge; a yellow belt around his body; and also red boots upon his feet. <note>The colors mentioned—gray, black, white, yellow, and red—correspond to the stages of the alchemical "Great Work."</note>

☞ After I had greeted him and come closer to him, I perceived that he held in his hand two *star-streaming* <note>original: *sternflüssige*; likely meaning "emanating from the stars" or "liquid-star."</note> flowers with seven rays—one was white, the other red. I asked the Farmer what he meant...

The **Philosophers**, however, for highly significant <note>original: "hochbedencklichen"; meaning deeply considered or weighty</note> reasons, have always written of the **first matter** <term>prima materia</term> in order to hide its root from the ignorant—and they have remained entirely silent regarding the **second matter** <term>secunda materia</term>. From it, you must extract **Man and Woman**; only then, **after their union** <term>post conjunctionem</term>, does it become the **first matter** <term>prima materia</term>, which I hereby faithfully reveal to you.

I marveled at these words and was also glad that he agreed with me on many points. ...no longer see nor find, etc. ☞ Truly, I did not know for joy what I should answer the farmer; yet I was heartily thankful and asked him further, quite kindly, whether there was anything more to be done here, and whether the **Art** <note>term: *Kunst* (Art) — in alchemical texts, "the Art" refers specifically to the practice of alchemy and the creation of the Philosopher's Stone</note> now had an end. ☞ He answered me very gently: "You should know that the virtue of these **lilies** <note>The Lily is a common alchemical symbol for the white, purified stage of the work, often associated with silver or the lunar "tincture"</note> can be renewed and increased every three days, so that they increase a thousandfold in their power and may cast their own seed. This happens when this seed is planted and cast into the previous earth; then, on the first day, their **darkness** <note>original: "Finsterniß"; this refers to the *nigredo* or blackening stage, where the matter must "die" before it can be reborn</note> shall occur; on the second day, the bright **moonlight** <note>original: "Monschein"; the *albedo* or white stage</note> comes; and on the third day, the **evening-red sun** <note>original: "Abend-röthige Sonne"; the *rubedo* or final red stage</note> emerges again. And this **Work** <note>original: "Werck"; the *Magnum Opus* or Great Work of alchemy</note> continues for as long as it pleases the Most High. Furthermore, from the nature of these flowers, precious stones and pearls also grow forth. ☞ But the highest thing, which concerns you humans for further knowledge of **GOD** and long life, is that if one partakes of even a single little leaf from this, he will soon recover from all lingering ailments <note>original: "Sieg-Tagen"; likely a corruption of "Siechtage," meaning days of sickness or infirmity</note> and illnesses. However, concerning their magical power and other high **secrets** <note>original: "Secretis"; Latin for hidden mysteries or specialized knowledge</note>, I cannot speak now; but when I come back to you after the completion of your work, then I will grant and share with you much higher qualities, properties, and circumstances of its virtue. For now, may you be content with this..."

<summary>The alchemical guide explains how the "Lily" (the purified matter) can be multiplied in potency through a three-day cycle of darkening and brightening. He also highlights the substance's medicinal power to cure all diseases and promises to reveal deeper magical secrets later.</summary>

"Oh my dear brother and best friend," I said, "you speak so excellently and thoughtfully of these high matters; it truly appears to me that you must be no common <note>term: *schlechter Bauer* — in Early Modern German, *schlecht* meant "simple" or "plain" rather than "bad"; the narrator is surprised that a humble laborer possesses such deep knowledge</note> farmer, even if you look like one from the outside. Furthermore, you also know magnificent Latin, which I am not accustomed to among farmers. Pray tell me, at which university did you learn such things? For you are very highly degreed, and I have never before encountered your like."
The farmer began to smirk and said, "What moves you to such a question?" I answered, "Because I would like to know if you learned such things at universities; for there they surely claim to possess the true **Philosophy** <note>In alchemical texts, "Philosophy" usually refers specifically to the Hermetic science or the knowledge of the Philosopher's Stone</note>."

Farewell. ☞ And as he said this to me, the farmer sprang and vanished hurriedly and swiftly into the mountain, and the two flowers remained and stood in the same place where the farmer had entered. I hurried toward them to pick them, but they yielded here and there before my hand; and as I hurriedly tried to reach out to seize them, ☞ behold, there lay a piece of several pounds of the right <term>raw and true matter of the Stone</term> <note>original: *crudæ & veræ materiæ Lapidis*</note> before me in their stead. ☞ And a voice came out of the mountain, saying: <term>God sells his goods for labor</term> <note>original: *Deus sua bona vendit laboribus*</note>; after that, I heard and saw nothing more.

I fell down there upon my knees, thanked and praised Him who lives from eternity to eternity, who is Wisdom itself; praying that He would fill and enlighten my heart, mind, and spirit with the Spirit of Wisdom, so that I might become a partaker of such a dear, high, and worthy treasure and obtain the same. ☞ I further promised and vowed to use and employ such a thing for the honor of His Holy Name, for the benefit of the Christian Church, and especially for the good and best of my neighbor and the dear poor.

Thus, my dear ones, you have now abundantly received the true foundation of the highest, dear, and worthy treasure with all its circumstances. # The Little Farmer.

...had given and done such a thing for highly important reasons / and also / so that the poor as well as the rich might have it / and would not have cause to complain or lament against **GOD** / as if **GOD** had given the rich a preference in this one thing / no truly / the rich do not respect it / and believe even less / that such a thing is hidden within it / as can be read in the *Great Rosary* <note>original: *Rosario Magno*; referring to the *Rosarium philosophorum*, one of the most famous illustrated alchemical treatises of the 16th century</note> folio 248: *“If we were to call our matter by its name, the foolish and the rich would not believe it to be so.”* <note>original Latin: *Si materiam nostram nomine nuncuparemus, insipientes & divites eam esse non crederent.* Alchemists often claimed their secret ingredient was a common, "vile" substance that people step on every day, such as salt, clay, or even waste.</note> Also the poor get it into their hands sooner than the rich / when I had now well wrapped and secured my <term>matter</term> <note>original: *materiam*; the physical substance used as the starting point for alchemical transmutation</note> / I went home again with joy / and sang the following little song on the way / from Chapter 7 of the Wisdom of Solomon:

# The Opened Cabinet of Nature.
<meta>catchword: no one</meta>

...caused one commotion after another in all the streets. The neighbors complained about me, fearing I would set their houses on fire. My friends and other acquaintances reproached me, saying that there were currently counterfeiters <note>original: *Müntzer*; literally "coiners." In this era, alchemists were often suspected of illegal minting or fraud.</note> active and about, and that I should abandon such worthless things and undertakings so as not to fall under suspicion. They urged me to earnestly pursue and attend to my legal profession <note>original: *facultatem Juridicam*</note> (since I was a Doctor of Law <note>original: *D. I.* for *Doctor Iuris*</note>), as I would otherwise be able to earn a plentiful living. ☞ But whether I could earn my bread with a good conscience that way, I unfortunately doubted very much. 

The next trouble was that the common smiths and goldsmiths complained about me before the City Council <note>original: *Rath*</note>, saying that I made the charcoal <note>original: *Kohlen*</note> too expensive for them. They claimed they could not carry out their trade or earn their civic livelihood, and consequently <note>original: *per Consequentiam*</note> would be unable to pay interest or taxes <note>original: *Schoß*; a traditional term for a property or capital tax</note> to the Council and the city. This was because I paid more for the charcoal so that I might secure it before they could. They kept this up for so long that the Council finally forbade me from doing so, and subsequently gave notice that I should cease from the hauling of charcoal <note>original: *à Tractione Carbonum*</note> and conduct myself according to my professional calling.

In short, the situation became so difficult that I had to tear down my furnaces <note>original: *Ofen*; the specialized brick ovens used for long-term alchemical heating</note>, move away from there, and seek a good friend who would provide me with the necessary funds, so that I could wait for the <term>Universal</term> <note>The "Universal Work" refers to the creation of the Philosopher's Stone, which was thought to cure all diseases and transmute any metal, as opposed to "Particular" works which only achieved limited results.</note> all the more quietly. (However, I never told...

<summary>The narrator faces social and legal persecution as his neighbors fear fire, his peers suspect him of counterfeiting, and local smiths blame his massive fuel consumption for rising charcoal prices. Forced by the City Council to stop his work, he decides to tear down his furnaces and relocate to continue his pursuit of the Universal Work in secret.</summary>

### The Little Peasant.
<meta>catchword: no one</meta>

(no one knew what I had in mind) that same **tribulation** <note>original: *Tribulation*; a period of great trouble or suffering.</note> with travels and other inconveniences lasted nearly until the third year. God knows how it grieved my heart that I knew it all, and yet could not set to work. I thought back and forth: perhaps God does not want it or will not permit it. **For where the fates drag us and pull us back, there we must follow.** <note>original Latin: *Nam quo nos fata trahunt retrahuntque, eo nos sequi oportet.* A quote from Virgil's *Aeneid* (5.709), suggesting that humans must submit to destiny.</note> Just as the **Count of Taruis** <note>Bernard Trevisan (1406–1490), a famous Italian alchemist whose writings describe his long, frustrating search for the Philosopher's Stone.</note> also indicates, that he had perfectly known the entire science of the **Universal** <term>Universalis: The "Universal Work," or the process of creating the "Great Elixir" that can cure all diseases and transmute any metal into gold.</term> two years prior, and yet because of many **impediments** <term>Impedimentorum: legal or physical obstacles.</term> and hindrances, he could not put it into practice. I commended all such things to the dear God.

☞ On my journey, I **conferred** <note>original: *conferirte*; to consult or hold formal discussions.</note> with learned people and became ever more knowledgeable; I **exchanged services** <note>original: *tractirete mutuas operas*; likely meaning he shared his legal or alchemical knowledge in exchange for the knowledge of others.</note> regarding the arts and sciences, as is the custom. I also gathered fine materials of all kinds of ores and hand-stones <note>Mineral specimens of a size that can be held in the hand, often kept in "cabinets of curiosity."</note>. But I found very few of them—indeed, no more than three—who walked and remained upon the true **philosophical path**. They all wanted to have to do with **common mercury** <note>original: *Mercurio vulgo*; ordinary quicksilver. Master alchemists often warned that common mercury was "dead" and could not be used to make the Philosopher's Stone.</note>, gold, **antimony** <term>Spießglas: the mineral stibnite, often used in alchemical processes to "purify" gold.</term>, **cinnabar ore** <note>The bright red mineral form of mercury sulfide.</note>, and many other lesser, more futile things. In this, they all erred greatly, because they did not work or follow according to Nature. Had they followed Nature and joined the right things together, they would not have erred so miserably. <meta>This page continues the author's argument from page 113 regarding the relationship between humanity and the natural world. # The Opened Cabinet of Nature #

An inexperienced person might object to you and say: from where does it come that **metals** <note>original: *Metalla*</note> have such a **sympathy** <note>original: *Sympathiam*; in alchemy, this refers to a natural, invisible resonance or attraction between different parts of the universe.</note>, **correspondence** <note>original: *Correspondenz*</note>, love, and friendship with humans, animals, and herbs? To their eyes, flesh and bone seem to **differ** <note>original: *discrepiren*</note> as much from metals and **minerals** <note>original: *Mineralia*</note> as heaven does from earth.

But such an argument is easily refuted if one first considers and compares the original **generation** <note>original: *Generation*; the process of being brought into existence or created.</note> of both humans and metals.

Man was not created by Almighty God out of a poor, impure, or common **clod of earth** <note>original: *Erdenkloß*</note>, as **ignorant theologians** <note>original: *ungelehrten Theologi*</note> and **shouters** <note>original: *Clamanten*; a derogatory term for those who preach loudly without true understanding.</note> believe. Rather, he was made from the very best, most refined extract, and so on, which existed in the **entire center of the earth** <note>original: *toto centro terræ*</note>.

And believe me: for such a work—which He created in His own image, and into which He breathed and planted a tiny spark or ray of His eternal **essence** <note>original: *Essenz*</note> and being—the Almighty took no common earth. Instead, as has been said, He took the **extract of the whole** <note>original: *ipsum extractum Totius*</note>, and then also

This shared origin justifies the use of metallic and mineral substances in treating human illness.</summary>

# NATURAL PHYSICS
# OF THE ROUND VISION
## CABALISTIC AND ALCHEMICAL. <note>original: "PHYSICA NATURALIS ROTUNDA VISIONIS CHYMICÆ CABALISTICA." The title suggests a synthesis of natural science, alchemy, and Jewish mystical traditions applied to a visionary experience.</note>

First, the Sun and Moon appeared, with the whole firmament <term>firmament: the vault of the heavens or the sky</term>; they stood still, and though they possessed their color, they gave off no light. Beneath them appeared a sphere that was earth-colored; in the center <note>original: "Centro"</note> there was again a small sphere, which was glistening snow-white. 

As I was observing all of this, there occurred a terrifying thunderclap with great lightning, which frightened me greatly; there was also a great mist. When this mist passed, there appeared above the Sun and Moon and the firmament a star that shone so brightly that I could not properly look at it; its color was redder than the sun is wont to be.

The pagans relied upon **astronomy** <term>Astronomia: At this time, astronomy and astrology were not yet distinct sciences; the term here encompasses both the study of celestial movements and the belief in their influence over human fate.</term> / they subjected themselves to the stars / and held the same to be their gods. You, however, should act and speak like human beings / not like the pagans and the irrational beasts. Let this be said to you and to everyone as a warning / so that God is not provoked / to undertake His punishment more strictly.

I asked: Is **astronomy** then nothing / and should one reject it entirely?

**Answer** <note>original: "Resp."; an abbreviation for the Latin *Responsum*, commonly used in scholarly dialogues.</note>: **Astronomy** is in itself a glorious art / which is to be praised and held in high regard / if one uses it correctly. However, it has become very false / as was said before / in that people want to make gods out of it. Secondly / the correct **calculation** <note>original: "Calculation"; the mathematical methods used to determine the positions and movements of celestial bodies.</note> is also lost / and current **astronomers** <note>original: "Astronomi"</note> only make do / as **Ptolemy** <note>Claudius Ptolemy (c. 100–170 AD) was an ancient Greek-Egyptian mathematician whose geocentric (earth-centered) model of the universe was the standard for over a millennium. By the time of this manuscript, his system was being challenged by Copernican heliocentrism.</note> described it / which is wrong and false /

<summary>The author argues that identifying oneself by astrological signs is a form of idolatry, asserting that humans are children of God rather than subjects of the stars. # Cabala Chymica #
<page-num>153</page-num>

...[heaven]ly growth it is, yet endowed with **metallic virtue** <note>original: *Metallischer Tugend* — the inherent power or quality that allows a substance to become or act upon a metal</note>, which an artist can extract: but this is the best, namely in Arabia and Hungary, for such [growth] carries the **excrements of rubies, carbuncles, and garnets** <note>original: *Excrementa Rubini, Carbunculi & Granatorum* — in alchemy, 'excrementa' refers to the refined secretions or residues of stones</note> within itself; it is red as blood and transparent. All others come from the stars and are, as has been said, nothing other than **excrements of the stars** <note>original: *Excrementa Stellarum* — a traditional alchemical belief that certain minerals or jellies (like Nostoc) were deposited on Earth by falling stars</note>, and of many kinds, such as black, green, yellow, white, and brown, according to the stars' type and nature.

Thus you can now recognize that the ancients erred regarding the **generation of metals** <note>original: *Generatione Metallorum*</note>, and it has been understood by no one as well as by **Hermes** <note>original: *Hermete* — referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of alchemy</note>. Since now, as stated, the **metals** <note>original: *Metalla*</note> grow and have a beginning, it follows from this that they must also multiply.

Now follows the other error of the ancients, where they claim that **gold** is eternal and indestructible; no, that is false: Every single thing has life and death within it; therefore, because gold has also received life, it has simultaneously inherited death as well. If this were not so, it could be destroyed neither by nature nor by art. **Experience** <note>original: *Experienz*</note>, however, has brought it to light otherwise...

# Nature’s Unlocked Chest #

The Art <note>In this context, "Art" (*Kunst*) refers to alchemy and the specialized knowledge of manipulating nature through divine wisdom.</note> which man has received from God is capable of all things; it stands far above Nature. This Art does not follow behind Nature as if it were her servant; instead, it rules over Nature, accomplishing and doing things that are impossible for Nature to do alone. For example: Man possesses within his knowledge the whole world—the Indies, **Spain** <note>original: *Hispaniam*</note>, and **Italy** <note>original: *Italiam*</note>. He also carries the entire heavens within him. Therefore, he can create in the northern regions <note>literally: "midnightly places"</note> that which Nature is only capable of producing in the southern regions <note>literally: "midday-like places"</note>. And again, what Nature produces in the East <note>literally: "the rising"</note>, man can make in the West <note>literally: "the setting"</note>. Thus, it is **conversely** <note>original: *é contra*</note> the case that man can prepare and bring forth a plant that grows in **India** in the middle of Norway. This is impossible for Nature to do because of the constraints of the firmament <note>The firmament refers to the spheres of the stars and planets, which were believed to dictate climate and the growth of specific plants.</note>; man, however, can imitate the power of the firmament. From the materials he chooses—be it stone or wood—he can prepare such a magnificent growth that Nature, in terms of power, virtue, form, and shape, would not dare to compare her own creations to it.

In the same way, man can produce Arabian gold in Germany, Sweden, or Poland; and conversely, he can produce Rhenish gold <note>Gold traditionally sourced from the Rhine River in Europe.</note> in Arabia. All of this is impossible for Nature alone.

This ability, however, arises from two parts:

Firstly, that God has created all things out of one single thing, and from that same thing the division was taken; therefore, they have a kinship with one another. This can be seen in the first man <note>A reference to Adam, who in the biblical tradition demonstrated his mastery and understanding of nature by naming all living creatures according to their true essence.</note>, who acted toward all things according to their virtues: ☞ Just as spirits were also ordained by God for this purpose, having received such wisdom from Him to reveal it to the most capable, and to those who are chosen for it. However, in the end, he dealt poorly with it <note>Referring to the biblical account of Solomon’s later turn toward idolatry.</note>. So it is also: even if this **seed** falls somewhat into the earth, it may easily be held back by something, so that it must remain stuck and there perish through the length of time.

Now, if the earth (as many **fantastical minds**—and **Aristotle** the most prominent among them—claim) were the **mother of metals**, one would not need to make much fuss; wherever the seed fell, it would soon return to its origin, or reach those **mercurial veins** <note>original: *mercurialische deren*; likely a variant of *Adern* (veins), referring to the underground channels where alchemists believed "mercury" or "living water" flowed to form minerals.</note> in which it sows itself and new ones grow forth. From this soul you first take the second, then the third soul; this happens through your subtle **preparation** <note>original: *Praparation*</note> and **purification** <note>original: *Purgation*</note>, which you partly know, but have not yet brought entirely to the end.

Thus, **rebirth** <note>original: *Wiedergeburt*</note> is a new **spiritual** <note>original: *Spiritualisches*</note> and **tempered** <note>original: *temperirtes*; in alchemy, this refers to a perfect balance of the four qualities—hot, cold, moist, and dry—so that no single element dominates or destroys the others</note> essence—a constant, spiritual, soulful, and super-celestial power; an undying and immortal force <image-desc>small printer's ornament</image-desc> which far surpasses the old essence. to bring the imperfect to perfection and thereby preserve it. Therefore, **rebirth** <note>original: *Regeneration*</note> consists of three parts.

Firstly: In the **killing of the body** / namely when it is **dissolved** <note>original: *resolvirt*</note> into the **first matter** <note>original: *primam materiam*; the primal, chaotic substance from which all things are made</note> / and made equal to it / that is / **vegetated** <note>meaning to be brought into a state of growth or potential</note>, and its sleeping **powers** <note>original: *Vires*</note> are awakened / and **brought into action** <note>original: *ad actum produciret*</note>, during which process its soul and spirit are also taken from it through this generation.

Secondly: In the **purification** of the body and the spirit: namely when the external / impure / destructible **elements** are taken from them / and in their place, the internal / invisible / hidden and indestructible / and **Heavenly elements** are given.

Thirdly: In the **union** <note>original: *Conjunction*</note>: When the pure soul and spirit are poured back into the pure **body** <note>original: *Corpori*</note> / and thereby it is made alive / and these three are united with one another / **clarified** <note>original: *clarificirt*; refined and made radiant</note>, and made constant and equally powerful. Therefore, **Hermes** <note>referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of alchemy</note> captured the entire **method** <note>original: *Methodum*</note> of the **Philosophical Work** <note>original: *Philosophischen Wercks*; the creation of the Philosopher's Stone</note> with the saying: **Take away its soul, and restore its soul** <note>original: *Aufer ei animam, & redde ei Animam*</note>: as well as with the phrase **Dissolve and coagulate** <note>original: *Solve & coagula*; the fundamental alchemical maxim of breaking down a substance to its essence and then rebuilding it in a pure form</note>.

The reason for this regeneration, however, is because **GOD** the **LORD**, on account of the terrible fall of **Adam** <note>original: *Adæ*</note>, has cursed the earth—that is, the elemental, corporeal, and lowest part of the world—and subjected it to corruption. And since **philosophical regeneration** <note>original: *Regeneratio Philosophica*</note> is nothing other than a **purification** <note>original: *Purgatio*</note> and separation of good and evil—the **soul** <note>original: *Anima*</note> being the highest part wherein life resides, followed by the **spirit** <note>original: *Spiritus*</note>, and finally the **body** <note>original: *Corpus*</note>, which is dead compared to the other two: So must the soul, as well as the spirit and body, first be separated from their perishable nature, so that subsequently these three, in the **philosophical work** <note>original: *opere Philosophico*; the process of creating the Philosopher's Stone</note>, become purely **souls** or **quintessences** <note>original: *quintæ essentiæ*; the "fifth element," the purest and most essential part of a substance</note>. 

For if one gives to the regenerated soul a body—whichever kind one desires—the body must follow the soul, and not the soul follow the **body** <note>original: *Corpori*</note>. One must understand, however, that the body must have a **symbolic affinity** <note>original: *Symbolum*</note> with that same soul, otherwise **anything would come from anything** <note>original: *ex quolibet quidlibet*; a Latin philosophical maxim meaning that without a shared nature or "seed," any substance could randomly turn into any other, which the author argues is impossible</note>. The reason for this is that life resides in the soul—and even more so an everlasting life in the regenerated soul—whereas the old body, by itself, is conversely

### Cabala Chymica.

...unspeakable **transparency** <note>original: *Diaphanitet*; the quality of being clear or translucent, often associated in alchemy with spiritual purity and the "glance" of the divine</note> called the Light, and which is also rightly named the First Water. Among all creatures, it possesses the perfect power and effect—indeed, it is a living fountain of all being. From its outward flow, everything below is refreshed, sustained, and quickened; therefore, it is also the Form, the **Action** <note>original: *Actio*; the active principle or force</note>, and the source of all other lower waters. Moreover, it has received the first and highest place next to God, through whose influence the Heavens, Stars, Planets, all **elements** <note>original: *Elementa*</note>, and **lower bodies** <note>original: *inferiora Corpora*; refers to physical matter on Earth</note> are maintained with their powers and movements. From this Light, other **separations** <note>original: *Separationes*</note> have now followed, one higher than the next, which were ordained by God for the creation of the Angels, the Souls, and also the preservation of the lower world. This Light is called the **empyrean heaven** <note>original: *cœlum Empyreum*; the highest, most celestial part of the universe, believed to be made of pure light</note> or the **life-giving heaven** <note>original: *vivificum*</note>, as well as the **superior or invisible world** <note>original: *Mundus superior vel invisibilis*</note>. Following this comes the second **separation** <note>original: *Separation*</note>, namely the threefold division—or the three distinctions—of the highest, middle, and lowest water, which signify the Soul, Spirit, and Body.

That is, it is a water that carries within it something of the light and the uppermost water, as well as the nature and properties of the lowest elemental water, acting like a <term>Sequester</term> <note>original: *Sequester*; a mediator or impartial third party</note>, a middleman and arbitrator. It must be impartial and inclined toward all parts, and also be able to reconcile itself with them according to each one's nature, and assume their perfect essence.

Secondly, just as this <term>Solution of the Microcosm</term> <note>original: *Microcosmi Solutio*; referring here to the dissolving of the "small world" or the starting material of the alchemical work</note> stood for a time and was ripened by the hovering Spirit of **God**, so that one thing could separate from the other: So too must the Philosophical <term>Solution</term> <note>original: *Solutio*; the process of dissolving a solid into a liquid state</note> be placed into <term>putrefaction</term> <note>original: *putrefactionem*; the stage of "rot" or decomposition which alchemists believed was necessary for rebirth</note> and be governed and corrupted by its own warm and moist spirit. This is done so that the <term>Body</term> <note>original: *Corpus*; the physical matter being worked upon</note>, through the spirit hovering within and above it, may be made spiritual, and thus body, soul, and spirit may rightly separate and divide from one another.

Thirdly, just as upon the ripening of the waters **God** the **Lord** undertook the <term>Separation</term> <note>original: *Separation*; the act of dividing pure from impure or light from dark</note> and divided them into four parts—namely into the light, and the uppermost, middle, and lowest waters—

# The Opened Chest of Nature

### in which the whole / upper and lower world / is contained as if in four **principal** <term>principal</term> <note>fundamental or primary</note> parts / and founded upon them, from which everything that lives / springs forth as if from a fountainhead: Thus the **Philosopher** <term>Philosophus</term> <note>A term for an alchemist or practitioner of natural philosophy</note> must also divide his whole work into four parts / as the main pillars of his artificial housing / namely into the Light / the uppermost / the middle-most / and the lowest water / and divide or separate these from one another.

## Preface to the Favorable READER.

Since in this last age of the world / and troubled, highly burdensome times / it is unfortunately found in deed and in truth / how severely and miserably [this gift] is despised and suppressed by the enemy of truth / through his instruments / with hatred / envy and mockery / this **divine and most precious gift** <note>original Latin: *Donum hoc Divinum & pretiosissimum*</note>, of the truest universal **spagyric medicine** <term>spagyric: an alchemical method of medicine-making that involves separating a substance into its components, purifying them, and recombining them to increase their potency</term>, of the **art and adept philosophy** <note>original: *artis & Philosophiæ adeptæ*</note>, in which alone that **great composition** <note>original: *magnum illud compositum*; the "Great Work" or the ultimate alchemical substance</note>—the power and ability of the upper and lower spheres <note>A reference to the Hermetic principle of "As above, so below," suggesting the medicine unites celestial and earthly forces</note>—rests in simplicity and is to be found / also enclosing and encompassing within itself the different qualities of all elements / **under the cabalistic number one** <note>original: *sub unario numero Cabalistico*; referring to the Monad or the idea of all things returning to a single, divine unity</note> / also in all illnesses and cures <note>original: *& curis*</note>, maintaining **victory and primacy** <note>original: *victoriam & principatum*</note> over all other medicines / to the honor and praise of God / who created them / from which alone the right / pure / most precious and truest **purple drinkable gold** <note>original: *Aurum potabile purpuratum*</note> of the new birth and regenerated being—which was called **Ambrosia and Nectar** <note>original: *Ambrosia & Nectar*; the food and drink of the Greek gods, here used to describe the life-giving properties of the alchemical elixir</note> by the ancients—has its origin / and is possible to obtain. For the benefit of the beloved and far-famed fatherland / in which **once** <note>original: *olim*</note> the **adept philosophy**, **by which the secrets of nature are explored** <note>original: *quâ naturæ secreta rimantur*</note>, flourished excellently / and now through

# The First Chapter.

It is found in **attained philosophy & the treasury of nature** <note>original: *Philosophia adepta & naturæ thesauro*; referring to the secret knowledge of those who have mastered alchemy</note> that three kinds of gold exist **in the nature of things** <note>original: *in rerum natura*</note>. The first is Astral; the second, Elemental; and the third, Metallic. **Paracelsus** the highly renowned, in his **Book of Vexation** <note>original: *Libello vexationis*; a well-known work by the Swiss alchemist Paracelsus (1493–1541)</note> agrees with this **opinion**, saying that likewise **gold is threefold** <note>original: *triplex esse Aurum*</note>: Firmamental, Elemental, and Metallic. **The Golden Fleece** <note>original: *Aureum Vellus*; likely referring to a collection of alchemical texts by that name</note> says that **the substance of gold is threefold: simple, composite, and doubly composite** <note>original: *triplicem esse substantiam auri, simplicem, compositam & decompositam*</note>, and many others **are of the same opinion**.

Now, however, it is not **specifically described** or reported by anyone among them all, which one in particular—as the best and most useful among the three—should be used or taken for the making of the **physicians' drinkable gold** <note>original: *aurum potabile Medicorum*; gold dissolved into a liquid state, believed to be a universal cure</note>. To **quash** and remove this **scruple** <note>original: *Scrupulum*; a small doubt or difficulty</note> and stumbling block along with all doubt, before one may proceed **to what lies beyond** <note>original: *ad plus ultra*</note>, it is necessary to know, when one wishes to undertake the **process** of **preparation**, that one first becomes well **informed** and learns **under what matter and form** the gold must have been created in its metallic essence and shape before one wishes to make it **medicinal** as a drinkable substance; thus it is found, according to the proven

# The Second Chapter.

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut drop cap for the letter D, featuring an ornate floral pattern and a small figure.</image-desc>

Now that the right **Subject, or the true roots of potable gold** <note>original: *Subjectum, vel veras radices auri potabilis*</note> have been found—which can be easily recognized from the aforementioned **discourse**—one shall realize that not the **body of gold** <note>original: *corpus auri*; the solid, metallic form of the element</note>, but rather the **First Being of gold** <note>original: *primum Ens auri*; the essential, spiritual precursor to the physical metal</note> should be used for the **preparation and solution of potable gold**. ## The Third Chapter.

The **Solution of Gold** <note>original: *Solutio Auri*; the process of dissolving solid gold into a liquid state</note> is held by all philosophers to be a great secret, indeed, likely the **highest secret of the spagyric art** <note>original: *summum arcanum spagiricæ artis*</note>. This is not without cause, because nothing more difficult is found in the <term>Sophic work (the alchemical "Work of the Wise")</term> than how it is possible to bring that which is **fixed** <note>original: *fix*; stable and resistant to fire</note> together with that which is **volatile** <note>original: *flüchtigen*; easily evaporated or transformed</note> into a proportional **solution** and **sublimation** <note>original: *Solution und Sublimation*</note>. This is something that no one will ever learn **by himself from reading or the study of philosophers** <note>original: *ex lectione vel Philosophorum studio per se*</note> alone, unless he has [attained] it through **manual operation and hidden faith** <note>original: *Manual operation & ocultata fide*</note> with mul- <note>the catchword *tiplici* indicates the word continues as *multiplici*, meaning manifold or repeated efforts</note>

For if gold could be **dissolved** <note>original: *resolvirt*</note> **by art or by this or that addition** <note>original: *arte vel additione hac vel illa*</note> and reduced into its **first being** <note>original: *primum esse*</note> and essence, it could also be born **anew** <note>original: *de novo*</note>. **Therefore** <note>original: *Ergo*</note>, it would not be fixed, but unstable, and in the **examination** <note>The process of testing the purity of a metal, often through intense heat</note> it would not withstand its trials as a fleeting thing. For **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurius*</note> is the most powerful centerpiece in the **generation** <note>The natural formation or "growth" of metals within the earth</note> of all metals, and nature must demonstrate her highest power in it before she is able to bring it out of **volatility** into **fixation** <note>The alchemical process of making a substance "fix," or stable and resistant to fire</note> through long years and time.

# Chapter Four.

Since in the previous [chapter] **mention** <note>original: *Mention*</note> was made of the materials through which the true **philosophical solution** <note>original: *Philosophische Solutio*; the alchemical process of breaking a substance down into its primary components</note> is possible, an extensive report shall now follow regarding from what **materials** such a **corrosive** <note>original: *Corrosiff*; a solvent or acidic agent used to dissolve metals</note> ought to be made, as current **laboratory workers** <note>original: *Laboranten*; practicing alchemists or chemists</note> stray far and **misuse** <note>original: *abusiren*</note> themselves therein.

--- Page 237 ---
<meta>This page continues the argument from the previous section regarding the principle of sympathy in alchemy. Having established that plant-based solvents should not be used for minerals, the author now provides authoritative citations to prove that metals must be dissolved by substances of their own nature—a concept often referred to as "like dissolves like."</meta>

<header>❦ [o] ❦</header>

<center>A corrosive dissolves the species of metals; yet only that which remains permanent—in matter and form, in species and form, according to its external and internal essence throughout every examination—is effective.</center>

**Lullius** <note>Ramon Llull (c. 1232–1316), a Majorcan philosopher and polymath to whom many influential alchemical works were attributed</note> confirms this in his *Testament*, folio 95: **"That every dissolved metal necessarily requires a lodging that is of its own proper matter, in which it is able to be born and multiplied."** <note>original Latin: *Quod omne metallum dissolutum indiget necessario hospitio, quod sit de propria ipsius materia in quo se habet nasci & multiplicari.*</note>

**Rupicissa** <note>John of Rupescissa (c. 1310–1366), a Franciscan friar and alchemist famous for his theories on the 'quintessence'</note> says: **"That no water dissolves metals by true reduction, except that which remains permanent in solidifications."** <note>original Latin: *Quod nulla aqua vera reductione metalla dissolvit, nisi illa, quae in congelationibus permanent.*</note>

**Albertus** <note>Albertus Magnus (c. 1200–1280), a Dominican friar and Bishop, recognized as one of the greatest medieval authorities on natural science</note> likewise mentions this in his treatise, chapter 8: **"That before all things, one must labor so that waters are made which possess within themselves the diverse qualities of the elements in power, through which they may awaken and solidify that which they wish to transmute, and by which the dissolved metals themselves can be solidified."** <note>original Latin: *Quod ante omnia laborandum sit, ut faciantur aquae quae habeant in se diversas Elementorum qualitates in virtute, per quas excitent & coagulant id, quod transmutare volunt, & qua ipsa metalla soluta possunt congelari.*</note>

**Augurellus** <note>Giovanni Aurelio Augurello (c. 1441–1524), an Italian humanist poet who wrote the famous alchemical poem 'Chrysopoeia'</note>, in book 1, says: Dissolve the **gold** in its own water; with this water, the ancients in former times **brought the ship into port.** <note>An alchemical metaphor meaning to successfully complete the Great Work or reach the desired result.</note>

A **Cabalistic author and philosopher** <note>Refers to the tradition of Jewish mysticism (Kabbalah) which was often synthesized with alchemy during the Renaissance to find hidden meanings in nature</note> writes: That there is only one thing in the whole world that dissolves gold and silver.

**Paracelsus** <note>The famous Swiss physician and alchemist (1493–1541) who pioneered the use of chemicals and minerals in medicine</note> in *On Long Life* and *On the Elixir*: **"Dissolve gold together with the substance of the corrosive of gold, and do so for as long as it takes until it becomes one and the same with the corrosive."** <note>original Latin: *Resolve aurum una cum substantia auri corrosivo, & id tantisper dum fiat idem cum corrosivo.*</note>
